```json
{
  "title": "Orchestrator-enforced completion: finishing sub-agent recovers stranded committed work",
  "team": "Personal",
  "project": "Autonomous cycle drain",
  "priority": 2,
  "description": "## What\n\nAs the drain-cycle operator running an unattended cycle, I want the orchestrator to drive a committed-but-unfinished issue to completion through a fresh finishing sub-agent before it halts, so that a weak worker that commits its slices but skips the finishing protocol no longer strands reviewable work or stops the run.\n\nThe seam lives in `_drain_one_issue` (`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`), at the two terminal halt sites: the not-Done halt (`:781`, slug `err-issue-not-done`) and the stack-no-PRs halt (`:706`, slug `err-stack-no-prs`). Before either reverts and records, the orchestrator inspects the worktree branch. With commits beyond base (`git rev-list <base>..HEAD --count > 0`) and the issue not properly closed, it spawns a finishing-only sub-agent — reusing `worker.run_issue` with a new `prompt.build_finishing` (alongside `_stack_preamble` / `_resume_directive`) — then re-reads `linear.get_issue` and `handoff.read`. On success it joins the existing happy path (`:733`): extend the baton, record submitted PRs, tear down. On failure it falls through to the unchanged halt, now naming that recovery was attempted.\n\n**Incident that motivated this:** in cycle `793fc9ea`, ABA-383 ran on a `model:haiku` worker that committed 4 reviewable slices but exited without running review → `/shape:pr-finishing` → handoff → Done. The not-Done halt reverted the issue and stranded the branch.\n\n## Why\n\nCompleteness is the orchestrator's contract to enforce, not the worker's to be trusted with. The orchestrator already reads the proof signals (`final_linear_state`, `handoff.read`); the only missing move is to act on a recoverable gap instead of halting on it. The finishing sequence is mechanical and was proven by hand on ABA-383, so a fixed **sonnet** sub-agent runs it reliably regardless of the issue's `model:` label, while any Critical/Required code fix — the part that needs capability — delegates to **opus**.\n\n**Rejected:** orchestrator runs `gh`/`gt` and posts the Linear comment itself — moves submission logic back into the orchestrator that AGENTS.md deliberately stripped it from. **Rejected:** trust uncommitted working-tree changes — completeness of an uncommitted tree is unknowable, so an empty or uncommitted-only branch stays a genuine failure and halts as today.\n\n## Completion\n\n- Done when: on the not-Done path, an issue left with commits beyond base and state ≠ Done triggers a sonnet finishing sub-agent; a post-spawn re-check reading Done continues the run, and still-not-Done halts with a reason naming the attempted recovery.\n- Done when: on the stack-no-PRs path, an issue Done with commits beyond base but no `pr_urls` triggers the same finishing sub-agent; a re-check reading non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton, and still-empty `pr_urls` halts.\n- Done when: an issue whose branch has no commits beyond base, or whose only changes are uncommitted, halts with the existing reason and spawns no finishing sub-agent.\n- Done when: the finishing sub-agent runs on `claude-sonnet-4-6` and its prompt instructs it to delegate Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`; at most one finishing attempt runs per issue per run; and a finishing spawn is recorded as its own run-log entry.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] **skeleton** — On the not-Done halt path in `_drain_one_issue`, detect commits beyond base, add `prompt.build_finishing`, spawn a sonnet finishing sub-agent via `worker.run_issue`, and re-check `linear.get_issue` + `handoff.read`, recovering to the happy path on Done or falling through to the existing halt (real nested `claude -p` spawn, not a stub). Done when: an issue exited not-Done with commits beyond base runs the finishing sub-agent and either reaches Done-and-continues or halts with a recovery-attempted reason, exercised end-to-end. *(Model: Opus)*\n- [ ] Extend the same recovery to the stack-no-PRs halt path (`err-stack-no-prs`): an issue Done with commits beyond base but empty `pr_urls` runs the finishing sub-agent and a re-check with non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton. Done when: a Done-but-no-`pr_urls` issue with commits beyond base is recovered to non-empty `pr_urls` and extends the baton, or halts unchanged when recovery fails. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n- [ ] Harden and bound the recovery — run the finishing sub-agent on `claude-sonnet-4-6` with a prompt that delegates Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`, guard one finishing attempt per issue per run, write a run-log entry for the finishing spawn, and enrich the fall-through halt reason. Done when: a second finishing attempt within one run is refused, the finishing spawn appears as its own run-log entry, and the prompt pins sonnet with an opus fix-delegation instruction. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n- **Finishing sub-agent loops / re-strands work.** Mitigation: one-attempt-per-issue-per-run guard; `max_resume_attempts` is the cross-run backstop.\n- **Finishing sub-agent re-implements rather than finishes.** Mitigation: `build_finishing` prompt states the work is already committed and forbids re-implementation. Falsifier: a finishing run whose post-spawn `<base>..HEAD` diff adds non-review commits.\n- **\"Committed beyond base\" misfires on an ambiguous base.** Mitigation: resolve `<base>` from the same value the worker prompt used and assert it is an ancestor of HEAD before spawning.\n\n---\n\nDelivery plan: `docs/delivery-plans/orchestrator-enforced-completion/` (passes `walk-delivery-plan` + `check-plan-framing`). Relates to the stacked-PR submission work (the same halt-and-chain machinery)."
}
```

With commits beyond base (`git rev-list <base>..HEAD --count > 0`) and the issue not properly closed, it spawns a finishing-only sub-agent — reusing `worker.run_issue` with a new `prompt.build_finishing` (alongside `_stack_preamble` / `_resume_directive`) — then re-reads `linear.get_issue` and `handoff.read`. On success it joins the existing happy path (`:733`): extend the baton, record submitted PRs, tear down. On failure it falls through to the unchanged halt, now naming that recovery was attempted.\n\n**Incident that motivated this:** in cycle `793fc9ea`, <issue id=\"96cabf59-da7d-4eb7-9032-7ea75663163a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-383/n08-reference-sweep-fresh-install-check\">ABA-383</issue> ran on a `model:haiku` worker that committed 4 reviewable slices but exited without running review → `/shape:pr-finishing` → handoff → Done. The not-Done halt reverted the issue and stranded the branch.\n\n## Why\n\nCompleteness is the orchestrator's contract to enforce, not the worker's to be trusted with. The orchestrator already reads the proof signals (`final_linear_state`, `handoff.read`); the only missing move is to act on a recoverable gap instead of halting on it. The finishing sequence is mechanical and was proven by hand on <issue id=\"96cabf59-da7d-4eb7-9032-7ea75663163a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-383/n08-reference-sweep-fresh-install-check\">ABA-383</issue>, so a fixed **sonnet** sub-agent runs it reliably regardless of the issue's `model:` label, while any Critical/Required code fix — the part that needs capability — delegates to **opus**.\n\n**Rejected:** orchestrator runs `gh`/`gt` and posts the Linear comment itself — moves submission logic back into the orchestrator that AGENTS.md deliberately stripped it from. **Rejected:** trust uncommitted working-tree changes — completeness of an uncommitted tree is unknowable, so an empty or uncommitted-only branch stays a genuine failure and halts as today.\n\n## Completion\n\n* Done when: on the not-Done path, an issue left with commits beyond base and state ≠ Done triggers a sonnet finishing sub-agent; a post-spawn re-check reading Done continues the run, and still-not-Done halts with a reason naming the attempted recovery.\n* Done when: on the stack-no-PRs path, an issue Done with commits beyond base but no `pr_urls` triggers the same finishing sub-agent; a re-check reading non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton, and still-empty `pr_urls` halts.\n* Done when: an issue whose branch has no commits beyond base, or whose only changes are uncommitted, halts with the existing reason and spawns no finishing sub-agent.\n* Done when: the finishing sub-agent runs on `claude-sonnet-4-6` and its prompt instructs it to delegate Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`; at most one finishing attempt runs per issue per run; and a finishing spawn is recorded as its own run-log entry.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] **skeleton** — On the not-Done halt path in `_drain_one_issue`, detect commits beyond base, add `prompt.build_finishing`, spawn a sonnet finishing sub-agent via `worker.run_issue`, and re-check `linear.get_issue` + `handoff.read`, recovering to the happy path on Done or falling through to the existing halt (real nested `claude -p` spawn, not a stub). Done when: an issue exited not-Done with commits beyond base runs the finishing sub-agent and either reaches Done-and-continues or halts with a recovery-attempted reason, exercised end-to-end. *(Model: Opus)*\n- [ ] Extend the same recovery to the stack-no-PRs halt path (`err-stack-no-prs`): an issue Done with commits beyond base but empty `pr_urls` runs the finishing sub-agent and a re-check with non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton. Done when: a Done-but-no-`pr_urls` issue with commits beyond base is recovered to non-empty `pr_urls` and extends the baton, or halts unchanged when recovery fails. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n- [ ] Harden and bound the recovery — run the finishing sub-agent on `claude-sonnet-4-6` with a prompt that delegates Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`, guard one finishing attempt per issue per run, write a run-log entry for the finishing spawn, and enrich the fall-through halt reason. Done when: a second finishing attempt within one run is refused, the finishing spawn appears as its own run-log entry, and the prompt pins sonnet with an opus fix-delegation instruction. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Finishing sub-agent loops / re-strands work.** Mitigation: one-attempt-per-issue-per-run guard; `max_resume_attempts` is the cross-run backstop.\n* **Finishing sub-agent re-implements rather than finishes.** Mitigation: `build_finishing` prompt states the work is already committed and forbids re-implementation. Falsifier: a finishing run whose post-spawn `<base>..HEAD` diff adds non-review commits.\n* **\"Committed beyond base\" misfires on an ambiguous base.** Mitigation: resolve `<base>` from the same value the worker prompt used and assert it is an ancestor of HEAD before spawning.\n\n---\n\nDelivery plan: `docs/delivery-plans/orchestrator-enforced-completion/` (passes `walk-delivery-plan` + `check-plan-framing`). Relates to the stacked-PR submission work (the same halt-and-chain machinery).","priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-393/orchestrator-enforced-completion-finishing-sub-agent-recovers-stranded","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-393-orchestrator-enforced-completion-finishing-sub-agent","createdAt":"2026-06-15T02:28:12.222Z","updatedAt":"2026-06-15T02:28:21.971Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Backlog","statusType":"backlog","labels":[],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Autonomous cycle drain - eliminate manual shepherding","projectId":"3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1"}

> AGENT

Done. Here's where things landed.

## ABA-383 — finished and Done
The stranded issue is closed out. Along the way I caught and fixed a real bug the haiku worker introduced: an `install.sh` `sed` substitution-order error that would have silently broken the nested `exec/*` command wrappers (their `@include` resolved to a non-existent path). Verified with an install/uninstall round-trip.
- **PR #47** opened, stacked on ABA-382/#46, attached to the issue
- Review-summary comment posted; issue transitioned to Done

## The orchestrator fix — shaped and filed
**[ABA-393](https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-393)** — *Orchestrator-enforced completion: finishing sub-agent recovers stranded committed work* (High, in the Autonomous cycle drain project, linked to ABA-383).

The design, with your decisions baked in:
- **Post-check at both halt sites** (`err-issue-not-done` and `err-stack-no-prs`): if the branch has commits beyond base but the issue isn't properly closed, recover instead of halting.
- **Recovery = a fresh finishing sub-agent** (reusing `worker.run_issue` + a new `prompt.build_finishing`) that runs review → `/shape:pr-finishing` → Done — the orchestrator never touches `gh`/`gt` itself.
- **Sonnet for the finishing spawn, opus for any Critical/Required fix.**
- **Both halt paths** covered; **uncommitted-only / empty branch = untrusted → halt** as today.
- Bounded to one finishing attempt per issue per run; `max_resume_attempts` stays the cross-run backstop.

Decomposed as one deliverable → one story node → skeleton + 2 slices (the skeleton exercises the real nested-spawn seam first). Delivery plan written to `docs/delivery-plans/orchestrator-enforced-completion/` and it passes both gates (`walk-delivery-plan`, `check-plan-framing`).

## Two things to note
1. The delivery-plan files under `docs/delivery-plans/orchestrator-enforced-completion/` are **uncommitted** (matching the existing untracked `watch-pane-rich-rendering/` plan). Want me to commit them to main?
2. ABA-393 is in **Backlog**, not assigned to a cycle — I didn't pull it into the current cycle to avoid silently expanding scope. Say the word if you want it slotted in.

```
{"id":"ABA-393","title":"Orchestrator-enforced completion: finishing sub-agent recovers stranded committed work","description":"## What\n\nAs the drain-cycle operator running an unattended cycle, I want the orchestrator to drive a committed-but-unfinished issue to completion through a fresh finishing sub-agent before it halts, so that a weak worker that commits its slices but skips the finishing protocol no longer strands reviewable work or stops the run.\n\nThe seam lives in `_drain_one_issue` (`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`), at the two terminal halt sites: the not-Done halt (`:781`, slug `err-issue-not-done`) and the stack-no-PRs halt (`:706`, slug `err-stack-no-prs`). Before either reverts and records, the orchestrator inspects the worktree branch. With commits beyond base (`git rev-list <base>..HEAD --count > 0`) and the issue not properly closed, it spawns a finishing-only sub-agent — reusing `worker.run_issue` with a new `prompt.build_finishing` (alongside `_stack_preamble` / `_resume_directive`) — then re-reads `linear.get_issue` and `handoff.read`. On success it joins the existing happy path (`:733`): extend the baton, record submitted PRs, tear down. On failure it falls through to the unchanged halt, now naming that recovery was attempted.\n\n**Incident that motivated this:** in cycle `793fc9ea`, <issue id=\"96cabf59-da7d-4eb7-9032-7ea75663163a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-383/n08-reference-sweep-fresh-install-check\">ABA-383</issue> ran on a `model:haiku` worker that committed 4 reviewable slices but exited without running review → `/shape:pr-finishing` → handoff → Done. The not-Done halt reverted the issue and stranded the branch.\n\n## Why\n\nCompleteness is the orchestrator's contract to enforce, not the worker's to be trusted with. The orchestrator already reads the proof signals (`final_linear_state`, `handoff.read`); the only missing move is to act on a recoverable gap instead of halting on it. The finishing sequence is mechanical and was proven by hand on <issue id=\"96cabf59-da7d-4eb7-9032-7ea75663163a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-383/n08-reference-sweep-fresh-install-check\">ABA-383</issue>, so a fixed **sonnet** sub-agent runs it reliably regardless of the issue's `model:` label, while any Critical/Required code fix — the part that needs capability — delegates to **opus**.\n\n**Rejected:** orchestrator runs `gh`/`gt` and posts the Linear comment itself — moves submission logic back into the orchestrator that AGENTS.md deliberately stripped it from. **Rejected:** trust uncommitted working-tree changes — completeness of an uncommitted tree is unknowable, so an empty or uncommitted-only branch stays a genuine failure and halts as today.\n\n## Completion\n\n* Done when: on the not-Done path, an issue left with commits beyond base and state ≠ Done triggers a sonnet finishing sub-agent; a post-spawn re-check reading Done continues the run, and still-not-Done halts with a reason naming the attempted recovery.\n* Done when: on the stack-no-PRs path, an issue Done with commits beyond base but no `pr_urls` triggers the same finishing sub-agent; a re-check reading non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton, and still-empty `pr_urls` halts.\n* Done when: an issue whose branch has no commits beyond base, or whose only changes are uncommitted, halts with the existing reason and spawns no finishing sub-agent.\n* Done when: the finishing sub-agent runs on `claude-sonnet-4-6` and its prompt instructs it to delegate Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`; at most one finishing attempt runs per issue per run; and a finishing spawn is recorded as its own run-log entry.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] **skeleton** — On the not-Done halt path in `_drain_one_issue`, detect commits beyond base, add `prompt.build_finishing`, spawn a sonnet finishing sub-agent via `worker.run_issue`, and re-check `linear.get_issue` + `handoff.read`, recovering to the happy path on Done or falling through to the existing halt (real nested `claude -p` spawn, not a stub). Done when: an issue exited not-Done with commits beyond base runs the finishing sub-agent and either reaches Done-and-continues or halts with a recovery-attempted reason, exercised end-to-end. *(Model: Opus)*\n- [ ] Extend the same recovery to the stack-no-PRs halt path (`err-stack-no-prs`): an issue Done with commits beyond base but empty `pr_urls` runs the finishing sub-agent and a re-check with non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton. Done when: a Done-but-no-`pr_urls` issue with commits beyond base is recovered to non-empty `pr_urls` and extends the baton, or halts unchanged when recovery fails. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n- [ ] Harden and bound the recovery — run the finishing sub-agent on `claude-sonnet-4-6` with a prompt that delegates Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`, guard one finishing attempt per issue per run, write a run-log entry for the finishing spawn, and enrich the fall-through halt reason. Done when: a second finishing attempt within one run is refused, the finishing spawn appears as its own run-log entry, and the prompt pins sonnet with an opus fix-delegation instruction. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Finishing sub-agent loops / re-strands work.** Mitigation: one-attempt-per-issue-per-run guard; `max_resume_attempts` is the cross-run backstop.\n* **Finishing sub-agent re-implements rather than finishes.** Mitigation: `build_finishing` prompt states the work is already committed and forbids re-implementation. Falsifier: a finishing run whose post-spawn `<base>..HEAD` diff adds non-review commits.\n* **\"Committed beyond base\" misfires on an ambiguous base.** Mitigation: resolve `<base>` from the same value the worker prompt used and assert it is an ancestor of HEAD before spawning.\n\n---\n\nDelivery plan: `docs/delivery-plans/orchestrator-enforced-completion/` (passes `walk-delivery-plan` + `check-plan-framing`). Relates to the stacked-PR submission work (the same halt-and-chain machinery).","priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-393/orchestrator-enforced-completion-finishing-sub-agent-recovers-stranded","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-393-orchestrator-enforced-completion-finishing-sub-agent","createdAt":"2026-06-15T02:28:12.222Z","updatedAt":"2026-06-15T02:29:24.175Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Backlog","statusType":"backlog","labels":[],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Ops - bugs, maintenance, emergent","projectId":"012923d7-25b5-475b-a1a7-5832a54ed389","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1"}
```

```
{"id":"812bc9b5-1618-48b8-8041-6d6223dc6d4c","icon":null,"color":"#bec2c8","name":"Multi-agent collaboration for correctness","summary":"Six agent-driven roles take hard tickets from intake to merged PR — task-shaping, outcome verification, structured PR prep, and autonomous PR-feedback response.","description":"**Goal:** For the operator running drain-cycle on outcome-focused Linear tickets, we want six agent-driven roles that take work from ticket to merged PR — task-shaping before code, outcome verification before Done, structured PR preparation, and autonomous response to human review comments — so that complex tickets don't silently land with missing AC, PRs are reviewable without guesswork, and human review feedback doesn't create a manual re-invocation tax.\n\n**Key results:**\n\n**KR1 (stretch)** — Of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow, ≥ 3 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits *(bet)*\n\n* baseline: 0 of 0 — none of the six roles exist as a connected flow today\n* target: ≥ 3 of 5 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits\n* measured over: the first 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow (\\~2 cycles at current cadence)\n* how we'll know: per-ticket grade markdown at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` (auto-drafted by `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>`, operator confirms draft → confirmed); exit code ≠ 0 on any violation\n\n*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*\n\n**KR3 (commit)** — Every PR submitted under the new flow has the structured What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge *(brake)*\n\n* baseline: PRs from drained tickets have whatever body the worker produced — typically a one-line subject and no review guidance\n* target: 0 PRs submitted under the new flow without either (a) structured What/Why/Focus body OR (b) auto-merge label with PR Preparer reasoning\n* measured over: every PR submitted under the new flow, for the initiative's duration\n* how we'll know: PR Preparer writes its decision to `prep_verdict` on the run-log entry; > AGENT
```

Reviewed ABA-393 against the **Multi-agent collaboration for correctness** project (6-role verify flow, M1–M5) and its 15 issues. Here's the assessment.

## Verdict: ABA-393 belongs *with* this project, not in Ops — and it collides with ABA-328 on the same code. It needs reconciliation before build, not a standalone drop-in.

## How it fits

The project's whole thesis is the orchestrator-side enforcement ABA-393 implements: *"complex tickets don't silently land… these checkpoints don't rely on ad-hoc agent judgement or get silently skipped."* ABA-393 is the missing **backstop** for exactly the failure the in-worker roles (ABA-327/328/330) are meant to prevent — a weak worker that skips its wired-in steps. It reads as a 7th role ("orchestrator drives completion when the worker didn't"), so Ops undersells it. That said, this project is in **Backlog** and hasn't started M3+, while ABA-393 is a live, already-incident-backed fix — so folding it in risks parking it behind a stalled initiative. That's the real trade-off for you to decide.

## Substantive findings

**1. Critical — ABA-393 and ABA-328 rewrite the same halt decision with opposite intent.**
ABA-328 ("Halt-on-fail: verify-flow ticket failing Outcome Verifier halts cycle with worktree intact") makes a verifier FAIL **stay halted**. ABA-393 makes a not-Done/no-PR exit **auto-recover** via a finishing agent. Both live in `_drain_one_issue`'s halt path. If built independently, ABA-393's recovery would spawn a finishing agent on a ticket the Outcome Verifier *deliberately rejected*, submit its PR, and drive it to Done — directly breaking **KR2 (zero silent Dones)**. ABA-393's detection must explicitly exclude the verifier-FAIL case: recover "committed-but-unfinished," never "committed-but-verifier-rejected." This is the one thing that must be designed jointly.

**2. Required — overlap with the PR Preparer (ABA-330 / KR3).** ABA-393's finishing agent runs `/shape:pr-finishing`; the project mandates every PR carry a What/Why/Focus body or auto-merge route via `/shape:pr-prepare` (ABA-330, KR3). A recovery that submits a PR while bypassing pr-prepare would violate KR3. The finishing agent's prompt should chain pr-prepare → pr-finishing, or the recovery should be scoped to invoke the same submission path the happy flow uses.

**3. Required — recording alignment (ABA-321 / KR2).** The project standardizes run-log fields (`outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, `responder_runs[]`) so the grader can audit uniformly. ABA-393's "finishing spawn run-log entry" should follow that schema (e.g. a `finishing_runs[]` field shaped like `responder_runs[]`), not an ad-hoc entry — otherwise `drain-cycle grade` can't see recovered Dones.

**4. Note — scope mismatch.** The project's roles are **verify-flow-gated** (only `verify`-labelled tickets). ABA-393's recovery is **not** flow-gated — it applies to every stack-mode drain. That's defensible (a safety net should be universal), but it means ABA-393 is broader than this project's contract; if folded in, decide whether recovery stays universal or gets gated to the verify flow.

## Recommendation

Re-home ABA-393 from Ops into this project under **M2** (it lives in the same worker-internal/halt-path layer as ABA-328), and add an explicit `relatesTo` ABA-328 + ABA-330 + ABA-321 with the reconciliation note from finding #1. Keep it High. If you'd rather not block it behind the backlog initiative, leave it in Ops but record finding #1 in the ticket so whoever builds it knows the verifier-FAIL exclusion is mandatory.

Want me to move it into the project + wire those relations, or just append the reconciliation note to ABA-393's description and leave it in Ops?

> DEVELOPER

yes do that, but also is any of what's been planned clash with the other ideas in that project? i planned it a while ago and don't quite remember all the details and whether or not i should proceed with it as it currently stands

> AGENT

I'll make the changes, then dig into the project's internal coherence. First, re-home ABA-393, wire the relations, and append the reconciliation note.

> TOOL

```json
{
  "id": "ABA-393",

  "description": "## What\n\nAs the drain-cycle operator running an unattended cycle, I want the orchestrator to drive a committed-but-unfinished issue to completion through a fresh finishing sub-agent before it halts, so that a weak worker that commits its slices but skips the finishing protocol no longer strands reviewable work or stops the run.\n\nThe seam lives in `_drain_one_issue` (`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`), at the two terminal halt sites: the not-Done halt (`:781`, slug `err-issue-not-done`) and the stack-no-PRs halt (`:706`, slug `err-stack-no-prs`). Before either reverts and records, the orchestrator inspects the worktree branch. With commits beyond base (`git rev-list <base>..HEAD --count > 0`) and the issue not properly closed, it spawns a finishing-only sub-agent — reusing `worker.run_issue` with a new `prompt.build_finishing` (alongside `_stack_preamble` / `_resume_directive`) — then re-reads `linear.get_issue` and `handoff.read`. On success it joins the existing happy path (`:733`): extend the baton, record submitted PRs, tear down. On failure it falls through to the unchanged halt, now naming that recovery was attempted.\n\n**Incident that motivated this:** in cycle `793fc9ea`, ABA-383 ran on a `model:haiku` worker that committed 4 reviewable slices but exited without running review → `/shape:pr-finishing` → handoff → Done. The not-Done halt reverted the issue and stranded the branch.\n\n## Why\n\nCompleteness is the orchestrator's contract to enforce, not the worker's to be trusted with. The orchestrator already reads the proof signals (`final_linear_state`, `handoff.read`); the only missing move is to act on a recoverable gap instead of halting on it. The finishing sequence is mechanical and was proven by hand on ABA-383, so a fixed **sonnet** sub-agent runs it reliably regardless of the issue's `model:` label, while any Critical/Required code fix — the part that needs capability — delegates to **opus**.\n\n**Rejected:** orchestrator runs `gh`/`gt` and posts the Linear comment itself — moves submission logic back into the orchestrator that AGENTS.md deliberately stripped it from. **Rejected:** trust uncommitted working-tree changes — completeness of an uncommitted tree is unknowable, so an empty or uncommitted-only branch stays a genuine failure and halts as today.\n\n## Completion\n\n* Done when: on the not-Done path, an issue left with commits beyond base and state ≠ Done triggers a sonnet finishing sub-agent; a post-spawn re-check reading Done continues the run, and still-not-Done halts with a reason naming the attempted recovery.\n* Done when: on the stack-no-PRs path, an issue Done with commits beyond base but no `pr_urls` triggers the same finishing sub-agent; a re-check reading non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton, and still-empty `pr_urls` halts.\n* Done when: an issue whose branch has no commits beyond base, or whose only changes are uncommitted, halts with the existing reason and spawns no finishing sub-agent.\n* Done when: the finishing sub-agent runs on `claude-sonnet-4-6` and its prompt instructs it to delegate Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`; at most one finishing attempt runs per issue per run; and a finishing spawn is recorded as its own run-log entry.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] **skeleton** — On the not-Done halt path in `_drain_one_issue`, detect commits beyond base, add `prompt.build_finishing`, spawn a sonnet finishing sub-agent via `worker.run_issue`, and re-check `linear.get_issue` + `handoff.read`, recovering to the happy path on Done or falling through to the existing halt (real nested `claude -p` spawn, not a stub). Done when: an issue exited not-Done with commits beyond base runs the finishing sub-agent and either reaches Done-and-continues or halts with a recovery-attempted reason, exercised end-to-end. *(Model: Opus)*\n- [ ] Extend the same recovery to the stack-no-PRs halt path (`err-stack-no-prs`): an issue Done with commits beyond base but empty `pr_urls` runs the finishing sub-agent and a re-check with non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton. Done when: a Done-but-no-`pr_urls` issue with commits beyond base is recovered to non-empty `pr_urls` and extends the baton, or halts unchanged when recovery fails. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n- [ ] Harden and bound the recovery — run the finishing sub-agent on `claude-sonnet-4-6` with a prompt that delegates Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`, guard one finishing attempt per issue per run, write a run-log entry for the finishing spawn, and enrich the fall-through halt reason. Done when: a second finishing attempt within one run is refused, the finishing spawn appears as its own run-log entry, and the prompt pins sonnet with an opus fix-delegation instruction. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Finishing sub-agent loops / re-strands work.** Mitigation: one-attempt-per-issue-per-run guard; `max_resume_attempts` is the cross-run backstop.\n* **Finishing sub-agent re-implements rather than finishes.** Mitigation: `build_finishing` prompt states the work is already committed and forbids re-implementation. Falsifier: a finishing run whose post-spawn `<base>..HEAD` diff adds non-review commits.\n* **\"Committed beyond base\" misfires on an ambiguous base.** Mitigation: resolve `<base>` from the same value the worker prompt used and assert it is an ancestor of HEAD before spawning.\n\n---\n\n## Relationship to this project (reconciliation — read before build)\n\nThis ticket is the **orchestrator-side backstop** for the same goal the six in-worker roles serve: completeness enforced, not trusted. It must be designed jointly with three siblings:\n\n- **ABA-328 (Halt-on-fail) — MANDATORY exclusion.** ABA-328 makes an Outcome-Verifier FAIL *stay halted*; this ticket makes a not-Done/no-PR exit *auto-recover*. Both rewrite the same halt decision in `_drain_one_issue`. The recovery here must fire **only on \"committed-but-unfinished\", never on \"committed-but-verifier-rejected\"** — otherwise it would submit and Done a ticket the verifier deliberately rejected, breaking **KR2 (zero silent Dones)**. Detection must read the verifier verdict and skip recovery on FAIL.\n- **ABA-330 (PR Preparer) — KR3 alignment.** The finishing agent submits via `/shape:pr-finishing`; KR3 requires every PR carry a structured What/Why/Focus body or auto-merge route via `/shape:pr-prepare`. The finishing prompt must chain pr-prepare → pr-finishing so recovered PRs satisfy KR3.\n- **ABA-321 (run-log schema) — recording alignment.** Record the finishing spawn in the standardized schema (a `finishing_runs[]` field shaped like `responder_runs[]`), so `drain-cycle grade` can audit recovered Dones uniformly.\n\n**Scope note:** this recovery is **not** verify-flow-gated today — it applies to every stack-mode drain. Decide at build whether it stays universal (a general safety net) or is gated to the verify flow to match the project's other roles.\n\nDelivery plan: `docs/delivery-plans/orchestrator-enforced-completion/` (passes `walk-delivery-plan` + `check-plan-framing`). Relates to the stacked-PR submission work (the same halt-and-chain machinery)."
}
```

> AGENT

I have what I need to answer the "should I proceed as-is" question. Here's the coherence review.

## Internal coherence: mostly sound, but stalled mid-build
Within the project, the six-role model still hangs together — no internal contradiction. But it's partially built and stopped ~2 weeks ago:
- **M1 (recording)** ~half done — ABA-322/323 done, ABA-320/321 still open.
- **M2 (worker loop)** ~67% — the Task Shaper skill + its worker invocation (ABA-327) and the interface ADR (ABA-337) landed; **ABA-328 (verifier halt path) is the key unbuilt piece**. ABA-326 (Task Shaper write-back) was **cancelled**, so that role is thinner than originally drawn.
- **M3/M4** untouched (0%).

So nothing self-contradicting — but it's a half-wired flow, which is the worst state to leave dormant.

## The real clash you're half-remembering: two newer projects reshape this one

**1. "drain-cycle supervises; the pack owns the workflow" (Planned, June 10) — direct structural conflict.**
That project's KR3 strips the worker prompt to a ≤15-line *pointer*, moving all workflow sequencing **out of drain-cycle and into the Shaper entry skill**. This project does the opposite for its roles: ABA-327 ("**worker session invokes** /shape:task"), ABA-330 ("**worker invokes** PR Preparer"), ABA-328 (halt path **in drain-cycle**) all wire role-sequencing *into* drain-cycle. Build them now and the supervisor re-scope rewrites or deletes them. These two projects are answering the same question — *where does orchestration live* — with opposite answers, and the supervisor one is sequenced "third of three" but was shaped more recently.

Worth noting: **ABA-393 (the ticket you just moved here) is actually supervisor-aligned** — orchestrator-owned recovery, not worker-internal. It fits the supervisor model more cleanly than this project's.

**2. Skill consolidation overtook the role inventory.**
This was planned May 28, before "Consolidate Shaper's Lifecycle into Four Phase Skills" (in progress — ABA-383 was its N08) and "Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills" (completed). Several of the six roles now **already exist as first-party skills**:
- Outcome Verifier → `shape:verify-implementation` ✅
- PR Preparer → `shape:pr-prepare` ✅
- Execution review → `shape:exec:review` ✅
- Task Shaper / PR Responder → folded or not-yet (`/shape:task`, `/shape:pr-respond` are stale pointers).

So the **skill-building half of M2/M3 is largely done elsewhere**; what's genuinely left is the drain-cycle *wiring + recording + grading*. Issues that reference `/shape:task`, `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`, `/shape:pr-respond` are pointing at a pre-consolidation map.

The project isn't *wrong*, it's *pre-dated*. One decision gates everything: **where orchestration lives** (worker-internal, this project's assumption, vs. supervisor-owned, the newer project's). Decide that first, because it determines whether ABA-327/328/330 survive in their current shape.

Concretely:
- **Keep and finish M1 (recording infra).** Run-log fields + grader are valuable under *both* models — it's the no-regret core. ABA-321 especially (ABA-393 depends on its schema).
- **Freeze M2+ pending the supervisor decision.** Re-inventory the six roles against skills that now exist; close/rewrite the stale-pointer issues.

> AGENT

Good instinct — the inconsistency is a vocabulary collision, not a real design conflict. Let me define it precisely, then inventory what actually exists.

## Defining "orchestration": two layers wearing one word

Both projects say "orchestration" but mean different layers. Pull them apart and the conflict dissolves.

**Layer 1 — Process supervision** (the *session* layer). Owns the lifecycle of a worker session: spawn it, enforce guardrails (token/turn caps, timeouts), halt on breach, revert Linear state, resume across runs, **recover stranded work**, record the run-log, grade. It is **content-blind and vendor-agnostic** — it doesn't know what "review" or "verify" *means*. It only reads artifacts: *Is the issue Done? Is there an `outcome_verdict`? Does `.drain-handoff.json` have `pr_urls`?* This is what `drain-cycle` should own.

**Layer 2 — Workflow orchestration** (the *intra-issue* layer). Owns the role *sequence* inside one issue: shape the task → implement → review → verify outcome → prepare PR → finish/submit → respond to feedback. It is **content-aware** — it knows the order and the gates. The supervisor re-scope says this belongs in the **Shaper pack** (an entry skill like `exec:pickup` that sequences the sub-skills), not inlined into `drain-cycle`'s worker prompt.

**The boundary is the artifact.** The supervisor and the workflow communicate only through durable signals — Linear state, the run-log fields, the handoff file. The supervisor *reads* those; the workflow *writes* them. Neither reaches across.

### Why this resolves the ABA-328 / ABA-393 collision
Both are **supervision** (Layer 1), not workflow:
- The *workflow* runs the Outcome Verifier and **writes** the verdict.
- The *supervisor* **reads** the verdict and decides: FAIL → halt (ABA-328); missing finishing artifact but commits present → recover (ABA-393); Done + `pr_urls` → proceed.

The supervisor never *computes* a verdict, only *acts* on one. So ABA-328 and ABA-393 are two branches of one post-check that reads signals — not two competing rewrites of the halt logic.

### The test for any ticket
> Does this require `drain-cycle` to know what a role *does* (Layer 2 → belongs in the pack), or only whether an artifact *exists* (Layer 1 → belongs in the supervisor)?

By that test, the "Multi-agent correctness" wiring tickets are mixed: ABA-321 (run-log schema) and ABA-328 (halt on verdict) are Layer 1 ✅; but ABA-327 ("worker session **invokes** /shape:task") and ABA-330 ("worker **invokes** PR Preparer") are Layer 2 — they put role-sequencing inside the supervisor, which is exactly what the re-scope pulls out.

```
1	"""Prompt builder for spawned ``claude -p`` sessions.
2	
3	The prompt is the entire contract between the orchestrator and the spawned
4	agent — there's no system prompt, no multi-turn loop. The four-segment
5	ordering below is load-bearing: the agent reads top-down, so context
6	(title + body) comes before instructions (preamble + tail), and the tail
7	line is last so it stays in the trailing-tokens window the model attends
8	to most strongly.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	_VERIFY_FLOW_LABEL = "verify"
16	
17	
18	def is_verify_flow(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
19	    """Return True when the issue carries the ``verify`` label."""
20	    return _VERIFY_FLOW_LABEL in issue.get("labels", [])
21	
22	
23	def _shape_task_directive(identifier: str) -> str:
24	    """Shape-task preamble for verify-flow worker sessions.
25	
26	    Instructs the worker to check for an existing Task Shaper output
27	    block in the Linear issue body before invoking ``/shape:task``. The
28	    check-first approach handles both fresh sessions (block absent →
29	    invoke) and §14 resumes (block present → reuse).
30	    """
31	    return (
32	        f"Shaping step (run before implementing): fetch the Linear issue body "
33	        f"for {identifier} and check for a Task Shaper output block "
34	        "(delimited by `<!-- TASK-SHAPER-START -->` and `<!-- TASK-SHAPER-END -->`). "
35	        "If the block is present, use it as your implementation guide and skip "
36	        "re-running `/shape:task`. If the block is absent, run "
37	        f"`/shape:task {identifier}` first and use its output as your "
38	        "implementation guide.\n\n"
39	    )
40	
41	
42	_TAIL = (
43	    "before marking Done: review the working-tree changes for correctness "
44	    "and quality, fix Critical/Required findings, commit + push, then post a "
45	    "review-summary comment on the issue and transition to Done."
46	)
47	
48	_STACK_TAIL = (
49	    "before marking Done: review the changes, fix Critical/Required findings, "
50	    "commit them as reviewable slices on the issue branch, then run "
51	    "`/shape:pr-finishing` to submit the stacked PR(s) and post the "
52	    "review-summary comment, and only then transition to Done."
53	)
54	
55	
56	def _resume_directive(identifier: str, base: str) -> str:
57	    """Resume preamble for a worktree carrying prior committed work.
58	
59	    Inserted as the first line inside the preamble (after the ``---``
60	    separator, before "Execution instructions:") so the agent reads it
61	    ahead of the procedure but the tail still holds the last-line
62	    position the four-segment ordering reserves for it.
63	
64	    The ``git log`` range is anchored at ``base`` so a chained worktree
65	    (branched off the prior issue's branch, not ``main``) reads back only
66	    its own commits rather than the whole stack beneath it.
67	    """
68	    return (
69	        f"Resuming issue {identifier}: this worktree carries prior committed "
70	        "work from an earlier session that was halted. Run "
71	        f"`git log --oneline {base}..HEAD` and `git status` first to read what "
72	        "is already done, then continue from that point — do not restart "
73	        "from scratch.\n\n"
74	    )
75	
76	
77	def _normal_preamble(
78	    identifier: str, worktree: Path, base: str, resume_segment: str, shape_task_segment: str
79	) -> str:
80	    return (
81	        "---\n\n"
82	        f"{resume_segment}"
83	        f"{shape_task_segment}"
84	        "Execution instructions:\n"
85	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
86	        f"- Base branch: {base}\n"
87	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
88	        "before marking Done):\n"
89	        "  1. Review the working-tree changes for correctness and quality.\n"
90	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
91	        "are at your discretion.\n"
92	        "  3. Commit and push to main.\n"
93	        "  4. Post a short review-summary comment on the Linear issue via "
94	        "`mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` (count of findings by severity, "
95	        "fixed vs deferred).\n"
96	        "  5. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
97	        '(state: "Done").\n'
98	    )
99	
100	
101	def _stack_preamble(
102	    identifier: str, worktree: Path, base: str, resume_segment: str, shape_task_segment: str
103	) -> str:
104	    # When the worktree is chained off a prior issue's branch rather than
105	    # ``main``, the slices stack on that branch — tell pr-finishing so it
106	    # slices ``<base>..HEAD`` instead of its ``main..HEAD`` default and
107	    # opens the PR against the right base. Omitted for the ``main`` base so
108	    # the unchained prompt stays unchanged.
109	    if base == "main":
110	        base_clause = ""
111	    else:
112	        base_clause = (
113	            f" These commits are stacked on `{base}`, not `main`, so pass "
114	            f"`{base}` to the skill as its base branch (it slices `{base}..HEAD`)."
115	        )
116	    return (
117	        "---\n\n"
118	        f"{resume_segment}"
119	        f"{shape_task_segment}"
120	        "Execution instructions:\n"
121	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
122	        f"- Base branch: {base}\n"
123	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
124	        "before marking Done):\n"
125	        "  1. Review the working-tree changes for correctness and quality.\n"
126	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
127	        "are at your discretion.\n"
128	        "  3. Commit the changes to the issue branch as reviewable slices — "
129	        "one logical change per commit. Do not push by hand.\n"
130	        "  4. Run `/shape:pr-finishing`. It submits the slices as stacked "
131	        "PR(s) via Graphite, writes the submitted PR URLs into "
132	        "`.drain-handoff.json` (`pr_urls`), and posts the review-summary "
133	        "comment on the Linear issue. Do not run `gt`/`gh` by hand or write "
134	        f"`.drain-handoff.json` yourself — the skill owns both.{base_clause}\n"
135	        "  5. Confirm `.drain-handoff.json` now contains a non-empty `pr_urls` "
136	        "list. If the skill could not submit, leave the issue In Progress and "
137	        "comment the blocker — do not mark Done.\n"
138	        "  6. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
139	        '(state: "Done").\n'
140	    )
141	
142	
143	def build(
144	    issue: dict[str, Any],
145	    worktree: Path,
146	    *,
147	    resumed: bool = False,
148	    stack: bool = False,
149	    base: str = "main",
150	) -> str:
151	    title = issue.get("title", "")
152	    description = issue.get("description") or ""
153	    identifier = issue.get("identifier", "")
154	
155	    resume_segment = _resume_directive(identifier, base) if resumed else ""
156	    shape_task_segment = _shape_task_directive(identifier) if is_verify_flow(issue) else ""
157	
158	    if stack:
159	        preamble = _stack_preamble(
160	            identifier, worktree, base, resume_segment, shape_task_segment
161	        )
162	        tail = _STACK_TAIL
163	    else:
164	        preamble = _normal_preamble(
165	            identifier, worktree, base, resume_segment, shape_task_segment
166	        )
167	        tail = _TAIL
168	
169	    return (
170	        f"# {title}\n\n"
171	        f"{description}\n\n"
172	        f"{preamble}\n"
173	        f"{tail}\n"
174	    )
175
```

Here's what the inventory shows — and it sharpens the answer considerably.

## What's actually in place

**The pack already owns the entire Layer-2 workflow.** `exec:pickup` is a built supervisor-pointer skill that sequences the whole intra-issue flow, each step delegated by name:

> pickup → `exec:breakdown` → `exec:build` → `exec:review` → `exec:verify` → `exec:finish`

Every role the "Multi-agent correctness" project planned to *build* already exists as a first-party skill:

| Project role | Skill in pack | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Task Shaper | `delivery` / `exec:breakdown` | ✅ exists |
| Outcome Verifier | `verify-implementation` / `exec:verify` | ✅ exists |
| PR Preparer | `pr-prepare` | ✅ exists |
| PR Finisher | `pr-finishing` | ✅ exists |
| Code review | `execution-review` / `exec:review` | ✅ exists |
| **PR Responder** | — | ❌ **MISSING** (only true gap) |

**But `drain-cycle` still inlines that same Layer-2 workflow.** `prompt.py` hard-codes the completion procedure as prose — `_normal_preamble`/`_stack_preamble` spell out "1. Review → 2. Fix → 3. Commit → 4. comment → 5. Done" (and a `/shape:pr-finishing` step), plus a `_shape_task_directive` that tells verify-flow workers to invoke `/shape:task`. So right now the workflow lives in **two places at once** — as `exec:pickup` in the pack *and* as procedure text in the supervisor's prompt. That duplication **is** the internal inconsistency you sensed, made concrete. (Note: that `/shape:task` directive points at a skill the consolidation superseded — `exec:breakdown` is the breakdown step now.)

## Mapping your scheduled projects to the two layers

- **"Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills"** (Completed) — built the Layer-2 `exec:*` skills. ✅ This is *why* the Multi-agent project's skill-building is now redundant.
- **"Consolidate Shaper's Lifecycle into Four Phase Skills"** (In Progress) — consolidating the shaping-side skills; ABA-383 was its tail.
- **"drain-cycle supervises; the pack owns the workflow"** (Planned) — the **keystone**: strip `prompt.py` to a ≤15-line pointer at `exec:pickup`, removing the inlined Layer-2 procedure. This is the project that *defines* the boundary and resolves the duplication.
- **"Multi-agent collaboration for correctness"** (Backlog) — predates all three. Its six-role build is overtaken; its **Layer-1 spine is still novel**.

## Verdict on "Multi-agent collaboration for correctness"

**Don't run it as planned — reframe it as the Layer-1 enforcement project.** Split its issues by the boundary test:

**Overtaken (close or fold) — these built/wired Layer-2 skills that now exist:**
- ABA-324 (build `/shape:task`), ABA-327 (worker invokes `/shape:task`), ABA-330 (worker invokes PR Preparer) — the skills exist; the invocation is `exec:pickup`'s job, not the worker prompt's. ABA-326 already cancelled.

**Keep — genuinely unbuilt Layer-1 supervision:**
- **Recording:** ABA-320/321/322 — run-log `flow` + verdict fields. Still needed; nothing writes verdicts into the run-log yet (`handoff.py` carries only `pr_urls`). There's an unbuilt seam here: how `exec:verify`/`exec:finish` verdicts cross the artifact boundary back into drain-cycle's run-log.
- **Enforcement:** ABA-328 (halt on FAIL) + **ABA-393** (recover on missing-finishing) — both supervisor post-checks reading verdicts. These are the same branch and should be built together, as the reconciliation note now says.
- **Grading:** ABA-333/334/336 (M5) and cost (ABA-335) — pure Layer-1.
- **PR Responder + `pr-feedback`** (ABA-332, M4) — the one missing skill plus its supervisor trigger.

## Recommended sequencing

1. **"drain-cycle supervises" first.** It establishes the pointer-prompt and the Layer-1/Layer-2 boundary. Everything else rides on that boundary being real.
2. **Then the reframed Multi-agent project as "Layer-1 correctness enforcement"** — recording fields → verdict-crossing seam → halt/recover post-checks (ABA-328 + ABA-393) → grading. Build the PR Responder skill (its only remaining Layer-2 piece) into the pack, not drain-cycle.
3. Its KRs survive almost unchanged — they're already phrased as artifact checks ("every Done has a recorded `outcome_verdict`"), which is Layer-1 by construction. Good sign the *intent* was always supervision; only the *implementation* drifted into the worker.

```
1	# Drain past the merge gate
2	
3	## Problem Statement
4	How might we let a drain run keep doing useful work — and keep turning hands-free — when the only thing left blocking it is a human review-and-merge gate on PRs it already opened?
5	
6	## Context
7	drain-cycle is transactional per issue: a worker marks a Linear issue **Done**, the orchestrator stacks its PR via Graphite, and continues. Within a run, downstream work stacks fine. The pain appears when the drain runs out of *runnable* work: anything still blocked by an **unmerged** dependency gets **deferred** (`linear.py`, external-blocker defer — `state_type not in _RESOLVED_STATE_TYPES`). The run ends, and the cycle lingers until the owner reviews + merges. The human merge serializes everything downstream.
8	
9	Desired end-state (from session): build on unmerged work, auto-merge *trusted* classes, and a loop that resumes as merges land — minimal owner touch points. Default trust posture is conservative (gate unless told otherwise).
10	
11	## Recommended Direction
12	A **three-gear** evolution, each independently shippable, smallest-blast-radius first. **The destination is a real resident daemon** — a drain-cycle process that stays alive, watches its own PRs, and will eventually host a full PR-review lifecycle (request review → ingest feedback → revise → re-submit → merge) as a long-running loop. The gears below are ordered so each one ships value on its own *and* lays a brick toward that daemon; a cron/`/loop` relaunch is an acceptable **interim** host for Gears 1–2 while the resident process is built, not the end state.
13	
14	**Gear 1 — Optimistic stacking (within-run).**
15	
16	*The problem in one picture.* Say the cycle has A → B → C, where B is blocked-by A and C is blocked-by B. Today:
17	- Worker finishes A, marks it Done, opens PR #1 (branch `aba-A`, based on `main`).
18	- Worker finishes B *only because* A is Done in Linear — B's branch `aba-B` stacks on `aba-A`, PR #2 opens.
19	- This already works **inside one run**. The trouble is the boundary: the moment a downstream issue is blocked by something whose PR is open-but-**unmerged** and that the run can't reach by stacking (e.g. it was deferred, in a later cycle, or a different repo), `linear.py`'s defer rule parks it because the blocker's `state_type` isn't "resolved". The drain then has nothing runnable and exits — even though the upstream *code exists on a branch* and could be built on.
20	
21	*The change.* Widen the "resolved" definition used by the defer rule: an upstream issue whose **PR is open** (optionally: open *and* green CI) counts as resolved-enough to unblock downstream. Then point the downstream worktree's base at the upstream **branch** (`aba-A`) instead of `main`, so the agent builds on the unmerged work. Concretely: a defer-policy tweak in `linear.py` + a base-branch selection tweak in the orchestrator's Graphite handoff (it already chains `last_branch_per_repo` for in-run stacks; this extends that to branches whose issues are Done-with-open-PR but not yet merged).
22	
23	*The cost it buys.* When A's PR changes in review, `aba-B`/`aba-C` were built on stale `aba-A` → they need `gt restack` and possibly re-execution. That rework risk is exactly the assumption to measure before committing (see below).
24	
25	**Gear 2 — Trust-tiered auto-merge.** A Linear label (e.g. `auto-merge`) marks issues whose PRs may merge themselves *only after* CI + `/code-review-and-quality` pass clean. Default: gated. For trusted issues the gate self-clears via `gh pr merge --auto` (or Graphite merge queue), so downstream merges naturally.
26	
27	**Gear 3 — Resident daemon (the destination).** drain-cycle becomes a long-running process that owns the full lifecycle without owner relaunch: it watches its own PRs, on merge it (a) `gt restack`s affected downstream and (b) picks up newly-runnable work; on review feedback it routes the PR back through a revise-and-resubmit loop. This is where the future PR-review lifecycle lives. Interim, before the resident process exists, Gears 1–2 can run under a cron/`/loop` relaunch of the existing transactional orchestrator — same observable behaviour for the merge-watch slice, far less to build — but that is scaffolding toward the daemon, not a substitute for it.
28	
29	## Key Assumptions to Validate
30	- [ ] **Review rarely invalidates upstream.** Measure: over the last N drained stacks, how often did review force upstream changes that would have invalidated optimistically-stacked downstream? If high, Gear 1 amplifies wasted work — kill it.
31	- [ ] **Trusted classes are reliably classifiable.** Test with a single conservative label on a few low-risk issues; confirm gated-by-default holds and CI+review gate is enforced before any auto-merge.
32	- [ ] **Interim relaunch is good-enough scaffolding.** Confirm a cron/`/loop` relaunch keeps the cycle moving while the resident daemon is built — i.e. it's a fine bridge, not that it replaces the daemon.
33	- [ ] **Graphite restack is safe unattended.** Verify `gt restack` after an upstream merge resolves cleanly often enough to run without a human; define the halt-and-park fallback for conflicts.
34	
35	## MVP Scope
36	Ship **Gear 2 on the interim relaunch host** first — removes the gate where it's safe with the least new logic, and de-risks the daemon before building its liveness:
37	- In: an `auto-merge` Linear label; after Done + green CI + clean review skill, the orchestrator enables `gh pr merge --auto`; existing drain relaunched via `/loop` or `CronCreate` so newly-unblocked work is picked up.
38	- Out (deferred to later gears): optimistic stacking on unmerged branches (Gear 1), automatic restack-on-review-change, and the resident daemon + PR-review lifecycle (Gear 3).
39	- Core assumption tested: does auto-merging trusted classes + relaunch clear the serialization barrier for *most* cycles? If yes, Gear 1's rebase risk may be unnecessary and the daemon can focus on the review lifecycle rather than unmerged-stacking.
40	
41	## Not Doing (and Why)
42	- **Resident daemon *first*** — it's the destination, but building liveness/restart-state/stop-hook handling before the merge-and-trust logic is proven inverts the risk order. Use the relaunch host to validate Gears 1–2, then build the daemon to own them + the PR-review lifecycle.
43	- **Optimistic stacking, initially** — highest rework-amplification risk; gate it behind the measurement above rather than building it on faith.
44	- **Auto-merge by default / label-alone** — the expensive failure is a risky PR on `main`; trust must be opt-in *and* CI/review-gated.
45	- **Hand-rolled merge queue** — Graphite has one; prefer it over bespoke serialization if Gear 2 needs queuing.
46	
47	## Open Questions
48	- Is "open PR" the right resolved-signal for Gear 1, or should it be "PR + green CI"?
49	- Trust as a Linear label, an issue-type, or a per-issue risk score from the review skill?
50	- For Gear 3, what's the merge-detection trigger — poll `gh`, a GitHub webhook, or piggyback on the existing cron pass?
51	- When a restack conflicts, halt-and-park the chain (current behaviour) or escalate differently?
52	
```

```
1	# Lifecycle expansion — shaping → execution
2	
3	Status: **confirmed and parked** — all three initiatives recorded in Linear (Personal team, Planned state) on 2026-06-10:
4	[A — Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/issues-drain-end-to-end-on-first-party-skills-be562671c8d8) ·
5	[B — Shaping consolidates to four dense front doors](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/shaping-consolidates-to-four-dense-front-doors-9c8a65552b93) ·
6	[C — drain-cycle supervises; the pack owns the workflow](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/drain-cycle-supervises-the-pack-owns-the-workflow-0f0222d99392)
7	Date: 2026-06-10
8	Inputs: deep review of obra/superpowers (5.1.0) and addyosmani/agent-skills (2e0dfbfb436e) installed plugins, audit of this repo, audit of ~/src/drain-cycle; interview-me session with confirmed intent.
9	
10	---
11	
12	## 1. Deep-review findings (condensed)
13	
14	### Shaper today
15	
16	14 skills (~3,086 lines), 2 hooks, 1 sub-agent (code-reviewer), 10 references, 3 rule files. Coverage is front-heavy by design: Define/Plan/Decide deep; **Build 0%, Deploy 0%, Ship partial** (pr-prepare, verify-implementation, code-reviewer). Three skills bust the pack's own 300-line cap: plan-review (369), initiative-shape (310), roadmap-shape (303).
17	
18	The "too lengthy" instinct, measured: median skill is ~225 lines — *between* superpowers (~190) and agent-skills (~290). What differs is density, not length: superpowers makes every section a gate, dot-graph, rationalization table, or prompt template, with 2 frontmatter fields vs Shaper's 10+ and no 1,700-line rules/references sidecar.
19	
20	### What the other packs have that Shaper lacks
21	
22	- **superpowers execution engine** — brainstorming → writing-plans (2–5 min TDD steps, exact code) → subagent-driven-development (fresh implementer subagent per task; two-stage review: *spec compliance before code quality*; never trust the implementer's report; built-in cheap/standard/frontier model routing) → finishing-a-development-branch. Worktree isolation as prerequisite. Three-layer verification: plan-step commands → per-task dual review → claim-level fresh-evidence gate (verification-before-completion).
23	- **agent-skills delivery loop** — /spec → /plan → /build (per-task RED/GREEN/commit) → /ship (parallel fan-out to code-reviewer + security-auditor + test-engineer personas → GO/NO-GO with mandatory rollback plan). Personas are ~100-line portable role prompts with a composition doctrine: skills = how, personas = who, commands = when; personas never call personas.
24	
25	### What neither pack has (Shaper's edge)
26	
27	Both are **tracker-blind and PR-blind**: no Linear, no Graphite, no stacked PRs, no auto-merge policy, no autonomous runner. Shaper + drain-cycle already own that territory — drain-cycle spawns workers per Linear issue in worktrees, routes models via labels, and its verify-flow already expects `/shape:task`, `/shape:verify-implementation`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`, with a half-built Graphite stack mode (`.drain-handoff.json`).
28	
29	**Conflict to resolve:** `drain_cycle/prompt.py` hardcodes `/code-review-and-quality` (an agent-skills skill). Uninstalling that pack breaks drains unless Shaper owns a replacement.
30	
31	---
32	
33	## 2. Confirmed intent (interview-me output)
34	
35	- **Outcome:** Shaper becomes the single pack covering the full lifecycle — `shape:*` for planning (4 front doors: `shape:idea`, `shape:project`, `shape:design`, `shape:delivery`) and a second verb namespace (e.g. `ship:*`, verb TBD) for execution: breakdown-at-pickup → TDD/incremental build → systematic debugging → simplification → reviewer fan-out → verify → Graphite-first PR finishing. superpowers and agent-skills get uninstalled.
36	- **User:** Headless drain-cycle workers first; interactive second. drain-cycle re-scoped to a thin vendor-agnostic supervisor (spawn, guardrails, halt, grade) whose prompt points at one skill — the workflow lives in the pack so a codex/kimi worker can follow the same prose.
37	- **Why now:** Planning half is deep but execution is 0%; drain-cycle's verify-flow half-expects skills that don't exist; two external packs fill the gap.
38	- **Success:** Both external packs uninstalled with nothing missed in practice; workers complete issues end-to-end on pack skills only; artefacts optimized for correctness and review-ease.
39	- **Constraints:** Linear for state; Graphite-first PRs (plain git fallback); sub-agents as portable persona prompt files (code-reviewer, security-auditor, spec-compliance) — Agent-tool dispatch on Claude Code, inline fallback elsewhere. Style reform: superpowers density, frontmatter cut to essentials, ~150-line target for execution skills, 3 over-cap skills brought under 300.
40	- **Pruning:** roadmap-shape, backlog-manage, app-calibrate deleted; idea-triage folds into `shape:idea`; spikes fold into `shape:design`; plan-review survives as the design/delivery exit gate; render-html survives as a utility.
41	- **Domain shortlist (in):** systematic debugging, code simplification, security-review persona. **(Out):** frontend/perf/browser set.
42	- **Out of scope this round:** deployment stage, implementer sub-agents (drain-cycle's job), cross-vendor adversarial review (future drain-cycle feature), renames (pack umbrella + drain-cycle).
43	
44	### Architecture decision: drain-cycle re-scope
45	
46	drain-cycle owns *processes* (cycle selection, worktree setup, vendor-agnostic worker spawning, token/time/cost guardrails, halt/resume, run logs/grading); Shaper owns *workflow* (the intra-issue procedure and reviewer personas). drain-cycle's inlined prompt procedure shrinks to "work issue X in worktree Y; follow the entry skill." Personas live as portable prompt files so non-Claude workers degrade to inline self-review with the same persona prompt. Cross-vendor adversarial review (Claude implements, codex reviews) becomes a later drain-cycle feature.
47	
48	---
49

51

55	|---|---|---|---|
56	| A | **Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills** | Execution namespace skills (~5–6), 2–3 personas, fixture harness + grader, one-line drain-cycle prompt pointer, validation drains | first — shaped below |

58	| C | **drain-cycle re-scope to thin supervisor** | Extract workflow from prompt.py, guardrail/grade cleanup, Graphite stack-mode completion, cross-vendor worker spawning | third — to be shaped |
59
```

=== first 25 lines ===
# Linear initiative — multi-agent collaboration for correctness

**Six agent-driven roles take a Linear ticket from intake to merged PR — so hard tickets don't silently ship with missing AC.**

**The bet.** drain-cycle today drains a Linear cycle unattended — one ticket, one worker, one diff. The next step: six agent-driven roles operating end-to-end on the same ticket — task-shaping before code, outcome verification before Done, structured PR preparation, and autonomous handling of human review feedback. Same operator, same tickets, less manual shepherding.

**What we'll know in 5 hard tickets.** Of the next 5 hard tickets drained under the new flow, the bet is ≥ 3 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits. Otherwise the kill condition triggers (§5) and contract enforcement reverts.

---

## 1 The problem and the bet

**Why this matters beyond drain-cycle.** drain-cycle is the batch driver for a growing toolbox of composable skills and MCPs. Each new skill compounds across both modes of use — at the keyboard during interactive work, and inside a worker session during a drain. This initiative ships the next four skills plus two CLI subcommands the toolbox needs to take a hard Linear ticket from intake to a merged PR, plus the durable contracts (run-log fields, Linear-issue dual-write, OTel attrs) that make their outputs inspectable afterwards.

drain-cycle evolves from "drain a Linear cycle unattended" toward a **UNIX-philosophy dispatcher for composable skill primitives** — Linear tickets as the work queue, skills+MCPs as the small tools, and `drain-cycle` itself as the batch-mode shell that composes them. The operator (you) drives the *why* (initiative goal + KRs) and the *what* (outcome-focused Linear issue); agents handle the *how* through a defined set of skills. The same skills run manually at the keyboard or automatically inside a worker session — design decision §10's symlink makes this true.

The operator failure mode this initiative addresses: **complex tickets that can't be sliced further** where the worker forgets details, under-verifies, or submits an unstructured PR — followed by human review comments piling up while the operator manually re-invokes the agent each round. Three concrete failures today:

1. **Silent Done on hard tickets.** Complex unsliceable tickets get drained; the worker self-asserts Done; the diff misses AC; the operator finds out at PR review and fixes by hand. *(e.g., a ticket asks for "halt the drain if Linear label resolution returns ambiguous matches"; the worker ships the label-parse change but never adds the halt logic; the diff passes self-review because nothing it touches asserts the halt-on-ambiguity contract.)* Left unaddressed, the unattended-drain promise breaks on the tickets where it matters most.
2. **Unstructured PR submission.** PRs land without What/Why/Focus guidance; the reviewer has to guess what to look at, or the operator manually rewrites the body. *(e.g., a multi-file refactor lands with a subject line and no body; the reviewer reads the diff blind to find out whether the risky bit is in `labels.py` or `worker.py`.)* Left unaddressed, PR review burden stays entirely on the human — auto-mergeable changes get the same scrutiny as risky ones, and review throughput suffers.
3. **Stalled feedback loops.** Human review comments accumulate on stacked PRs; the operator manually re-invokes claude each round. *(e.g., a stacked PR gets three rounds of review comments across two days; each round the operator opens the worktree, re-launches claude, and shepherds the fix manually.)* Left unaddressed, the agent ecosystem stops at code-submission time instead of running end-to-end through merge.

The bet: six agent-driven roles end-to-end, each backed by a skill (or CLI subcommand) fitting the UNIX-philosophy frame. A drained ticket flows through the pipeline below; the per-role detail table follows.
